# NOTES: Companies Act 2006 (United Kingdom), part coa7: Part 13, Resolutions and meetings (ss 281 to 361), lines 3717-4655 of `ukpga-2006-46.txt`

Written by part agent coa7 (Claude Sonnet 5.5), 2026-11-12, on top of the keel's `coa-core.l4` and `coa-definitions.l4` (read-only copies). Status: every module in this folder compiles on the stable binary 1.6.110 (main, e51181d, 2026-11-11) and on 1.6.109 (main, 4c887e0, 2026-11-04) with no `DiagnosticSeverity_Error` or `_Warning`; every assertion is satisfied; no coverage row is left `deferred`. Not reviewed by a lawyer.

## Source and what "verified" means here

The only source is `ukpga-2006-46.txt`, a plain-text rendering of the Act from legislation.gov.uk (Open Government Licence v3.0) made by a converter script (`inputs/SOURCES.md`, text version 2026-07-16). It is not the official text, and nothing here is "verified against the official text". Every `-- src:` quote line was generated by `srcrefs.py quote` from that file, and `python3 -I srcrefs.py check --strict ukpga-2006-46.txt coa7-*.l4` reports 0 problems (the figures are in "What check.sh printed" below). The text file must be named on that command line: it is not part of this folder. Line numbers are of the file as it is.

## Seat

The individual who is, or is about to become, a director, a company secretary or a member (shareholder) of a company registered under the Act, or who deals with one, asking on facts they can state what the Act requires or lets them do, what rights they have against the company or its directors, what they must disclose, file, approve or vote on and by when, and what follows (liability, offences, penalties) if it is not done. (Assumed, not ruled: the Act is addressed to companies, so the individual is whoever stands in a company's management or ownership.) Courts, the Registrar and the State appear only as parties the text names, never as a seat. The seat layer is `coa7-seat.l4` (170 questions, each beginning "Under section N,") with `coa7-cases-seat.l4` (462 assertions); the questions are listed in "The seat layer" below.

## What is encoded and what is not

Encoded: every provision of lines 3717 to 4655, ss 281 to 361 of Part 13 (Chapters 1 to 7), as 898 rows in the coverage table below (871 text units and 27 defined terms). Layer 1 is 385 decision functions (and the types they need) in ten modules that follow the Act's chapters (`coa7-ch1` Chapter 1; `coa7-ch2` Chapter 2; `coa7-ch3a`, `coa7-ch3b`, `coa7-ch3c`, `coa7-ch3d` Chapter 3 in four parts; `coa7-ch4`; `coa7-ch5`; `coa7-ch6`; `coa7-ch7`, with ss 360 to 361), plus `coa7-regulative.l4` (five duties with a time limit as regulative rules), `coa7-tests-ch*.l4` (1317 assertions) and the seat layer. Not encoded as rules: s 354 and s 344(4) (the Secretary of State's regulation-making power and the procedure for regulations: machinery, inert reason 2), headings (inert reason 1), and four rows of dots (repealed or blank). Amounts and descriptions the Act leaves to regulations or orders are inputs: the regulation-specified connection of s 344(1)(c); the prescribed fee of s 358(4); the places specified under s 1136 (s 358(1)(b)); a level on the standard scale is never turned into pounds.

Where this brief and `inputs/PLAN.md` differ the brief governs. The two differences found: (1) PLAN.md section 4.2 names the answers other parts take as `s 282 the resolution is an ordinary resolution`, `s 283 the resolution is a special resolution`, `s 288 the resolution is a written resolution`; the layer-1 functions that give those answers are named with their subsections, `s 282(1) the resolution is an ordinary resolution`, `s 283(1) ...`, `s 288(1) ...` (see "Seams (coa7)"); (2) PLAN.md says nothing about the seat layer's names; the brief requires "Under section N," and every question has it.

## Coverage (coa7)

Dispositions: `encoded` (717 rows), `fork` (142 rows: encoded, and the row points at the fork-register entries it rests on), `inert` (35 rows: reason 1 for the 24 headings; reason 2 for s 344(4) and the rows of s 354), `repealed or blank in this text` (4 rows: s 303(3), s 327(2)(c), s 330(6)(c), s 360B(5)). There is no `out-of-scope` and no `deferred` row.

**Audit.** `work/cover.py` (a script that reads the text, not shipped) counted 939 non-blank lines in lines 3717-4655 (all 939 lines are non-blank) and built one row per heading (24), section (96), subsection (347), paragraph (367), sub-paragraph (37) and defined term (27): 898 rows. Every non-blank line falls in exactly one unit row (0 lines without a row). The table is built from the text by script, so the audit found no unit to add: 898 rows before and after. What it found was 4 rows with no function (`s 319(2)`, `s 328(2)`, `s 344(4)`, `s 346(1)`); after the repairs none is unmapped: `s 346(1)` got its own function (`s 346(1) this section applies to the appointment`, two tests), `s 344(4)` is inert reason 2, and `s 319(2)` and `s 328(2)` ("subject to any provision of the company's articles") are the articles input of the functions for ss 319(1) and 328(1). A row whose function is that of its enclosing provision says so in the note column and shows no test count of its own. "Tests" counts the `#ASSERT` lines of `coa7-tests-ch*.l4` that call the function(s) of the row.

### Reading a result: FALSE and NOTHING (coa7)

The rule of 2026-10-29 (assumed, not ruled by a lawyer) as it reads in Part 13. Almost every function answers `MAYBE BOOLEAN`; a few answer `MAYBE NUMBER` (a number of days, votes or a percentage), `MAYBE DATE`, a `MAYBE` of a named choice, or `MAYBE Penalty`.

- `JUST TRUE`: the Act says the case is within the rule (a resolution is passed, a request is enough, a duty applies, an article is void).
- `JUST FALSE` ("a no"): the Act says no in its own words: a "means" or "is" definition (`s 288(1)`, `s 298(2)`, `s 345(2)` to `(5)`, `s 360C`); a condition the text introduces with "if", "where", "when", "once", "unless", "in the event of" or "only if" that is not met; a period the text fixes in its own words that has been missed ("not more than 21 days after", "at least 14 days"); a flat negative ("may not be revoked", "is not required", "does not affect", "need not be paid"). Each such branch carries `-- FALSE-kind (the text says no in its own words): "<the quoted words>"`.
- `NOTHING` ("not covered"): the Act does not decide the case: it lists without closing the list ("includes", "the following"), leaves a term undefined ("simple majority", "officer or employee" of a non-auditor), is silent, points outside the sources (the articles, Part 15, section 1121, regulations, another Act), or limits a grant or prohibition in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit ("a statement of not more than 1,000 words", "the members of a private company", "a company other than a traded company"). Each such branch carries `-- NOTHING-kind (<why>)`, <why> being one of silent, list not closed, includes, undefined term, points outside the text, limited by its own wording.
- A caller must never turn `NOTHING` into `FALSE`. `REFUSE` is not used: "not covered" is `NOTHING` or a named choice, asserted in the tests of every module.
- A seam answer another part computes is an input: its `JUST FALSE` is the other part's no, and its `NOTHING` passes through as `NOTHING`.
- Two kinds of answer that look like a "yes" are not: an **"unless" bar** is encoded as the bar (decided case by case, in the shape ruling (d) of 2026-11-05 gives the "until" bar): `JUST TRUE` says the thing is barred, `JUST FALSE` says the bar is lifted and never that the thing is valid; and a **safe harbour** that fails answers `NOTHING` (fork F10).

The rulings of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05 recorded in the brief govern this table: **E1** (ruled 2026-11-04: option B as guidance for gathering a legal effect's routes by hand only; option C, per-section answers, applies everywhere else, so no function here gathers one legal effect from several sections, and the layer-1 functions for ss 282, 283, 288 and 360C are the per-section answers other parts take); **N2** (ruled 2026-11-04, option A: flat negatives count as a no, and the Hohfeld kinds stay in the jural-relations table below); the **periods, "where" and scope-limit** ruling (a period fixed in the text's words and a "where" condition answer `JUST FALSE` when missed; a scope limit with no "only" answers `NOTHING`); the **delegated definitions, defences and "until" bars** ruling of 2026-11-05 ((a) a definition handed to a list answers JUST FALSE when no head holds; (b) a defence not proved answers JUST FALSE for that provision's defence; (c) "when" counts as "where"; (d) an "until" bar is encoded as the bar, never as "has passed"); and the **case-by-case** ruling (the maintainer, typed 2026-11-05T10:25:24Z): the maintainer's words, "In short I feel like these decisions need to be made case-by-case at encoding time, and you should go with your gut most of the time; and if the encoding turns out to be wrong, well, that's where the human review comes in." The case-by-case sites each have a fork line below (F1, F5, F6, F8, F10, F15 to F19, F21).

## Fork register (coa7)

Each entry gives the reading taken, the other reading, and the text that licenses each; the sites are generated from the `-- FORK Fn` tags in the modules (137 sites). Marginal readings (the two readings the brief leaves "for a lawyer") are in their own section, not here. The ids run F1 to F22 with no F9: the number was held spare while writing and no entry took it, and the tags in the modules carry the ids as given.

### F1: a bare "must be passed" not done (s 281(1), (2))

- **Taken:** a resolution of a private company passed by neither of the two ways of s 281(1), or of a public company not passed at a meeting, answers JUST FALSE ("must be passed— (a) ... or (b) ...").
- **Other reading:** NOTHING, because s 281(4) saves "things done otherwise than by passing a resolution" and the other law on when a resolution is treated as passed (the Duomatic line of cases is outside the sources).
- **Licence:** s 281(1)-(2) "must be passed" against s 281(4).
- **Sites (2), one line each (provision, function, file:line):**

| provision | function | answer taken | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| s 281(1) | `s 281(1) a private company's resolution is passed in a way this section allows` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:34 |
| s 281(2) | `s 281(2) a public company's resolution is passed in the way this section requires` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:46 |

### F2: "simple majority" is not defined (s 282(2) to (4))

- **Taken:** a simple majority is more than one half; exactly half is not a majority (JUST FALSE).
- **Other reading:** NOTHING for exactly half (an undefined term), or at least half.
- **Licence:** s 282(1) "passed by a simple majority", with no definition anywhere in the Act's ranges; s 283 by contrast says "not less than 75%".
- **Sites (3), one line each (provision, function, file:line):**

| provision | function | answer taken | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| s 282(2) | `s 282(2) a written resolution is passed by a simple majority` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:121 |
| s 282(3) | `s 282(3) a resolution passed on a show of hands is passed by a simple majority` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:131 |
| s 282(4) | `s 282(4) a resolution passed on a poll is passed by a simple majority` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:141 |

### F3: an "is not ... unless" bar lifted by a statement (s 283(3)(a), (6)(a))

- **Taken:** encoded as the bar only (decided case by case under the 2026-11-05 ruling on "unless" once met, in the shape ruling (d) gives the "until" bar): JUST TRUE says the resolution is not a special resolution; JUST FALSE says the text does not bar it (it stated, or the notice included and specified, what the section asks). The functions never say the resolution is a special resolution: that is s 283(1) with the vote counts.
- **Other reading:** encode positively ("is a special resolution" when the statement was made).
- **Licence:** s 283(3)(a), (6)(a) "is not a special resolution unless".
- **Sites (2), one line each (provision, function, file:line):**

| provision | function | answer taken | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| s 283(6)(a) | `s 283(6)(a) the resolution passed at the meeting is not a special resolution` | JUST FALSE | coa7-ch1.l4:236 |

### F4: "subject to" the articles, an enactment or another section

- **Taken:** where the text yields to the articles, an enactment or another section, the function for the rule answers JUST FALSE when that yielding applies (the rule does not have effect), JUST TRUE when it does not; for a number that the articles would fix, NOTHING (it is fixed outside the sources).
- **Other reading:** NOTHING throughout: the articles and the other section are outside what the text decides.

### F5: a bare "must" not done (the 2026-11-05 ruling, case by case)

- **Other reading:** NOTHING: a bare "must" says what is required and does not say "no" to anything else.

- **Other reading:** NOTHING: lifting an exception does not itself say the company is bound.
- **Licence:** "it is not bound to comply with that section unless there is deposited with or tendered to it ... a sum reasonably sufficient".

### F7: counting days, weeks, months and years (the Act has no general counting rule)

- **Taken:** "not more than N days after D" and "within N days from D" are D plus N (the last day counts); "the period of N days (months, years) beginning with D" runs from D itself, so it ends the day before the same day N later (28 days beginning 1 March end 28 March; two years beginning 1 March 2026 end 29 February 2028; ten years from 1 March 2016 end 28 February 2026); "16 days beginning with the day of the meeting" ends 15 days after it; "three months after" is `add months` (clamped to the end of a short month).
- **Other reading:** the day after (D plus N plus one) or the anniversary itself; the clear-day rule of s 360 is not extended beyond the ten provisions it lists.
- **Licence:** s 360 lists ten provisions only; no other section says how to count.

### F8: "not ... unless" encoded as the bar (ss 299(2), 309(1), 312(1), 330(2), 330(3), 350(1), 358(2))

- **Taken:** TRUE says the bar holds (the resolution or notice is not valid, the resolution is not effective); FALSE says the bar is lifted, never that the thing is valid or effective (decided case by case, in the shape of ruling (d) of 2026-11-05). For s 350(1) and s 358(2), where the "unless" lifts a duty or an offence, JUST FALSE says the offence or duty does not arise on those facts.
- **Other reading:** encode the positive (valid when available throughout).
- **Licence:** "is not validly sent ... unless", "is not effective unless", "does not affect ... unless", "commits an offence unless", "unless they have at all times been kept".

### F22: a function named for a grant or a power whose "if" gate fails (name-test sweep of 2026-11-12)

- **Taken:** NOTHING: the text grants the power or permission on a condition it introduces with "if" and does not close with "only", so a case outside the gate is outside the grant, not the text saying no (the Guide's sentence on a grant "limit[ed] ... in its own wording", applied as the lead's name-test sweep instructs).
- **Other reading:** JUST FALSE: a failed "if" read as the text's own no (marginal reading (a); the Guide's other sentence lists a condition introduced with "if" among the cases that answer JUST FALSE).
- **Licence:** s 305(1) "If the directors ... do not do so ..., the members ... may themselves call a general meeting"; s 306(2) with s 306(1) "This section applies if ..."; s 307(4) "may be called by shorter notice ... if shorter notice is agreed by the members"; s 337(2) "may be called by shorter notice ... if all the members ... agree".

### Name-test sweep of 2026-11-12

The lead's sweep read every layer-1 function whose name contains "may", "permitted", "power", "entitled" or "authorised" (69 functions). A function named for a grant or a power whose answer was `JUST FALSE` because an "if" gate failed now answers `NOTHING` (fork F22 above: one line, the other reading named). Where the text closes the gate itself ("only", "may not", "unless", "provided that") or the function is a bar, a duty or the provision's own question, `JUST FALSE` stays. Two groups are listed and not decided, as instructed: "may" functions whose gate is "where", "when" or "upon" (ruling (b) of the periods ruling says `JUST FALSE`; the Guide's line on grants points the other way), and "entitled" functions (an entitlement is a claim, not a power). This part had made no earlier decision of this kind to keep.

| flipped to NOTHING | kept JUST FALSE (gate closed by the text) | listed: "may" with where/when/upon | listed: "entitled" |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | 21 | 8 | 5 |

The other 31 of the 69 functions are not touched: each already answers `NOTHING` (or a value) outside its gate and has no `JUST FALSE` branch from a failed gate.

**Flipped to `NOTHING` (4):** `s 305(1) the members may themselves call a general meeting`; `s 306(2) the court may order a meeting to be called, held and conducted in any manner the court thinks fit`; `s 307(4) the meeting may be called by shorter notice than otherwise required`; `s 337(2) the annual general meeting may be called by shorter notice`. Their sites, assertions restated and the other reading are under F22; each is also in the marginal readings below as a branch resting on (b).

**Kept `JUST FALSE` (21):**

- `s 283(3)(b) the written resolution may only be passed as a special resolution`: "may only be passed" (the text's own "only")
- `s 283(6)(b) the resolution passed at the meeting may only be passed as a special resolution`: "may only be passed" (the text's own "only")
- `s 286(1) the holder's own vote may be counted by the company`: "only the vote of the senior holder ... may be counted"
- `s 286(1) a vote by a proxy duly authorised by the holder may be counted by the company`: "only the vote of the senior holder ... (and any proxies duly authorised by him) may be counted"
- `s 292(2) the resolution may properly be moved as a written resolution`: "may properly be moved ... unless (a) ... (b) ... or (c)": the text states the exceptions itself
- `s 296(3) the member's agreement may be revoked`: "once signified, may not be revoked" (a flat negative)
- `s 303(5) the resolution may properly be moved at the meeting`: "may properly be moved ... unless": the text states the exceptions itself
- `s 322A(2) the provision may be made subject to the requirement or restriction`: "may be made subject only to such requirements and restrictions as are"
- `s 324(2) the member may appoint more than one proxy in relation to the meeting`: "provided that each proxy is appointed to exercise the rights attached to a different share": a proviso, read as "only if" (the one reading in this column that is an inference)
- `s 327(A1)(b) the company may require the evidence`: "but may not require to be provided with anything else"
- `s 338(2) the resolution may properly be moved at the annual general meeting`: "may properly be moved ... unless": the text states the exceptions itself
- `s 338A(2) the matter may properly be included in the business at the annual general meeting`: "may properly be included ... unless": the text states the exceptions itself
- `s 343(3) the directors may appoint the person`: "The directors must not appoint a person who": a bar; silence stays NOTHING
- `s 344(1) the person may not be appointed as an independent assessor`: "A person may not be appointed": a bar, not a grant
- `s 348(3) the right conferred by this section may be exercised`: "only to be exercised to the extent"
- `s 349(4) the statement may be used in evidence against the person`: "may not be used in evidence against him ... except" (a flat negative)
- `s 360A(2) the use of electronic means may be made subject to the requirement or restriction`: "may be made subject only to such requirements and restrictions as are"
- `s 360A(3) subsection (2) affects the company's power to require reasonable evidence of entitlement`: "Nothing in subsection (2) affects": the provision's own question, a flat negative
- `s 315(1) the company must send a copy of the statement to each member entitled to receive notice of the mee...`: a duty ("must send"); "entitled" only describes the recipient
- `s 339(1) the company must send a copy of the resolution to each member entitled to receive notice of the an...`: a duty ("must send"); "entitled" only describes the recipient
- `s 340A(1) the company must give notice of the matter to each member entitled to receive notice of the annua...`: a duty ("must give notice"); "entitled" only describes the recipient

**Listed, not decided: "may" with where, when or upon (8):** one row each, in the table below.

**Listed, not decided: "entitled" (5):** one row each, in the table below.

#### Listed, not decided: "may" functions whose gate is "where", "when" or "upon"

The question for the maintainer or a lawyer: does a failed "where" gate on a grant answer `JUST FALSE` (ruling (b), and ruling (c) of the 2026-11-05 delegated ruling), or `NOTHING` (the Guide's line on a grant limited by its own wording)? Answered today as `JUST FALSE`; the other reading is `NOTHING`.

| function | the gate | what it answers today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `s 285(4) subsection (3) authorises the proxies together to exercise these rights` | "Where a member appoints more than one proxy" | JUST FALSE for a member with one proxy; the branch for more extensive rights ("does not authorise") is the text's own no and stays JUST FALSE |
| `s 298(1) the company is deemed to have agreed that the document or information may be sent by electronic me...` | "Where a company has given an electronic address in any document containing or accompanying a proposed written resolution" | JUST FALSE when that gate, or "relating to that resolution", fails; a deeming rule is listed here because its name carries "may be sent" |
| `s 306(3) the court may give ancillary or consequential directions` | "Where such an order is made" | JUST FALSE when no order was made under subsection (2) |
| `s 307A(7) the adjourned meeting may be called by shorter notice` | "Where a general meeting is adjourned" | JUST FALSE for a meeting that is not adjourned; the branch "in the case of an adjournment for lack of a quorum this subsection applies only if" is closed by the text and stays JUST FALSE |
| `s 333(1) the company is deemed to have agreed that the document or information may be sent by electronic me...` | "Where a company has given an electronic address in a notice calling a meeting" | JUST FALSE when the gate, or "relating to proceedings at the meeting", fails; listed as s 298(1) is |
| `s 333(2) the company is deemed to have agreed that the document or information relating to proxies may be s...` | "Where a company has given an electronic address" in an instrument of proxy or an invitation to appoint a proxy | JUST FALSE when the gate, or "relating to proxies for that meeting", fails; listed as s 298(1) is |
| `s 358(4) the member may require a copy of any of the records` | "on payment of such fee as may be prescribed" (read as "upon payment") | JUST FALSE when the fee has not been paid |
| `s 358(7) the court may by order compel an immediate inspection of the records or direct that the copies req...` | "In a case in which an inspection ... is refused or a copy ... is not sent" (read as "where") | JUST FALSE when neither has happened |

#### Listed, not decided: "entitled" functions

The question for the maintainer or a lawyer: is an entitlement a claim whose failed gate answers `JUST FALSE`, or a grant whose failed gate answers `NOTHING`? The Guide does not settle it. All five answer `NOTHING` outside their gate today, so nothing was changed; a ruling of `JUST FALSE` would change them.

| function | the text's words at the gate |
| --- | --- |
| `s 323(2) the person is entitled to exercise the same powers on behalf of the corporation as an individual m...` | "A person authorised by a corporation is entitled to exercise" |
| `s 324(1) the member is entitled to appoint the person as his proxy` | "A member of a company is entitled to appoint another person as his proxy" |

## Marginal readings for a lawyer (coa7)

The brief marks two readings "for a lawyer, not for you to decide", and asks that every branch that rests on one be listed with the other reading stated. The readings are taken as the status quo.

- **(a)** A failed "if" counts as the text's no, clause by clause, within the provision that holds the "if" (an inference: "if A then B" does not itself say "if not A then not B"), and a flat negative counts as the text saying no. **The other reading:** the branch answers `NOTHING`, because the text states a condition or a negative and does not say what follows when it fails.
- **(b)** A prohibition or grant limited by its own wording does not count as the text's no, so the case outside the limit answers `NOTHING`. **The other reading:** the branch answers `JUST FALSE`, because the limit is the text's own no to the case outside it.

**How the table is made.** It is generated from the `-- FALSE-kind` and `-- NOTHING-kind` tags in the modules (423 branches: 215 on (a) and 208 on (b)). A branch rests on (b) when its tag says `limited by its own wording`. A `JUST FALSE` branch rests on (a) unless its quoted words state a period (the periods ruling of 2026-11-05 governs those), a definition (`means`, `has the meaning`) or a numeric threshold the text states (`%`, `not less than`, `at least`, `more than one half`); those three kinds are not marginal. A branch tagged by hand `-- MARGINAL a` or `b` is listed as tagged. The other kinds of `NOTHING` (silent, list not closed, includes, undefined term, points outside the text) are not marginal and are not listed. The list is over-inclusive on purpose: a lawyer who rules (a) or (b) the other way finds every branch it moves in one place.

## Jural relations (coa7)

Hohfeld's correlatives, stated once per provision as a relation between two parties and named neutrally; each relation is encoded once, by the rule named in the last column, and never again from the other side. A provision that creates no relation (a definition, a counting rule, a rule of evidence, a saving, a condition of validity) is listed once as `none`. 194 subsection-level rows create a relation and 167 are `none`. Where the text names the party only as "the company", "the directors", "a member", "an officer in default" or "the court", that is the party shown; the State is the party with the power to prosecute an offence the Act creates.

## Seams (coa7)

Each seam input a function of this part takes from another part, as PLAN.md section 4 names it (taken as `MAYBE BOOLEAN` unless stated), and each answer this part gives for others.

**Inputs taken (the lead wires the answering part's function to each):**

- `s 1121 the officer is in default` (coa24): every offence function (`s 291(5)`, `s 293(5)`, `s 311A(8)`, `s 315(3)`, `s 325(3)`, `s 326(3)`, `s 336(3)`, `s 339(4)`, `s 340A(3)`, `s 341(3)`, `s 343(4)`, `s 351(3)`, `s 355(3)`, `s 358(5)`); an officer who is not in default answers `JUST FALSE`, and a `NOTHING` input answers `NOTHING`.
- `s 168 the resolution removes a director before the expiration of his period of office` (coa4) and `s 510 the resolution removes an auditor before the expiration of his term of office` (coa12): `s 288(2)`.
- `s 971(1) the company is an opted-in company` and `s 969 the meeting is held by virtue of section 969` (coa21): `s 307(A1)`.
- `s 385 the company is a quoted company` (coa8): `s 361 the company is a quoted company` (coa7-ch7) returns it; `s 341(1)`, `s 342(1)` and `s 352(1A)` (coa7-ch5) take it as an input of the same name, `s 361 the company is a quoted company`, which the lead wires to that function.
- `s 1136 the place is specified in regulations under section 1136` (coa25): `s 358(1)(b)`.
- Taken as plain facts, not as seam inputs, because the person can state them: membership (s 112, coa3: "the person is a member of the company"); a provision of the articles (s 18, coa1: "the company's articles ..."); a public company (s 4(2), keel: `the company is a public company`); a traded company (this part, s 360C); the accounting reference date (s 392, coa8) and the last day of the financial year (s 390, coa8) as dates; hours of a period that fall on a day that is not a working day (s 1173(1), keel); "authenticated" (s 1146, coa25), "in hard copy form or in electronic form" (s 1168, keel), "prescribed" (s 1167, keel); parent and subsidiary undertakings (s 1162, keel) for `s 344(3)`; "treasury shares" (s 724, coa15) and "paid up" (s 583, coa13) as the figures the Act's percentage tests use.
- Cross-references to ss 152 and 153 (coa3) in ss 284(5), 314(2), 338(3), 338A(3) and 342(2) ("See also section 153") are signposts: the figures the seat states are the members' own holdings, and a member who holds on behalf of others states the figure the nominee rules produce.

**Answers given to other parts (named as PLAN.md section 4.2 names the seam; the layer-1 function that gives it is named with its subsection):**

- `s 282 the resolution is an ordinary resolution`: `s 282(1) the resolution is an ordinary resolution` (way of passing, a `s 282 vote count`).
- `s 283 the resolution is a special resolution`: `s 283(1) the resolution is a special resolution` (same inputs).
- `s 288 the resolution is a written resolution`: `s 288(1) the resolution is a written resolution`.
- `s 289`, eligible members: `s 289(1) the member is an eligible member`; `s 290` circulation date: `s 290 the circulation date` (a list of dates).
- `s 312` special notice: `s 312(1) the resolution is not effective` (the bar: `JUST TRUE` when notice of the intention was not given 28 clear days before the meeting).
- `s 360C the company is a traded company`: `s 360C the company is a traded company` (a list of `s 360C class of shares`); `s 361`: `s 361 the company is a quoted company`.
- **Seam the plan lacks:** PLAN.md lists "annual general meeting | s 336" among the expressions this part answers for others; s 336 requires a meeting and does not define one (finding 17), so there is no yes/no answer to "is this meeting an annual general meeting". `s 336(1)` and `s 336(1A)` answer whether one was held in the period; a caller that needs "is this an AGM" takes it from its own facts. Another gap: s 337(1) and s 307(2)(a) use "annual general meeting" only as a fact about the notice.

## Findings (what the text turns out to say)

1. Four subsections or paragraphs are shown blank in this text, each a row of dots: s 303(3) (line 3902), s 327(2)(c) (4184), s 330(6)(c) (4212) and s 360B(5) (4641). Three more show omitted words inside a live line: s 286(2) ("register of members ...", 3778), s 311(3)(b)(i) (4011) and s 334(2)(a) ("303 to 305), . . .", 4248); the list in s 360(1) also has an omitted entry after "section 338(4)(d)(i)" (4607). They are rows `repealed or blank in this text` or part of the row they sit in; no rule is encoded for the missing words.
2. Subsection numbers are not always plain integers: (A1) and (A2) come before (1) in s 307; (A1) before (1) in ss 327 and 330; (1A) and (1B) in ss 336 and 341; (2A) in s 334; the sections themselves run 285A, 307A, 311A, 319A, 322A, 324A, 333A, 338A, 340A, 340B, 360A, 360AA, 360B, 360BA, 360C. Each is its own row and its own function, named with the Act's numbering.
3. The Act has no general rule for counting days. Only s 360 says how to count, and only for the ten provisions its subsection (1) lists (clear days: the day of the meeting and the day notice is given are excluded). Everywhere else "within 21 days from the date" and "not more than 28 days after" are counted by the plain difference (fork F7). For a provision that is not on the s 360(1) list the Act gives no counting rule at all.
4. s 297(1) counts "the period of 28 days beginning with the circulation date": the circulation date is day 1, so the last day is 27 days later (1 March: 28 March). s 341(1B)(a) "the end of 16 days beginning with the day of the meeting" ends 15 days after the meeting.
5. Two different "requisite percentage"s: s 292(5) (5%, or a lower figure in the articles) and s 307(6) (90%, or a higher figure not above 95%, for a private company; 95% for a public company). Both are defined terms with a row and a function.
6. s 353(1) calls the information in s 351 "report of independent observer to be made available on website"; s 351 itself says "independent assessor" and its heading is "Information to be made available on website". The encoding follows s 351. s 352(1A) says "The provisions of section 342 to 351" (the plural is missing): no effect.
7. s 321 has two different limbs: (1) a provision of the articles is void if it excludes the right to demand a poll on any question other than the chairman's election or an adjournment; (2) a provision is void if it makes ineffective a demand by 5 members, 10% of the voting rights or 10% of the paid-up sum "on any such question". They are two functions, not one.
8. s 350(4)(b) gives different maxima by place: England and Wales twelve months, Scotland and Northern Ireland six months. A conviction "on summary conviction" with no place named is not covered: `NOTHING` (the core's `Mode of conviction` has a value that says only "on summary conviction").
9. The Part's offences all follow one pattern ("an offence is committed by every officer of the company who is in default" and a penalty subsection); the penalties are three: indictment fine / summary statutory maximum (ss 291, 293, 315, 336, 339, 340A); summary level 3 (ss 311A, 325, 326, 341, 350(2), 351); level 5 (s 343); level 2 (s 357); level 3 with a daily default fine of one-tenth of level 3 (ss 355, 358); and s 350(4) with imprisonment. Each is a function of `Mode of conviction` returning the core's `Penalty`; the pounds a level stands for are not in the Act.
10. The 48-hour and 24-hour cut-offs (ss 322A(3), 327(2), 330(6), 360B(1)(b), (2)) are measured "taking no account of any part of a day that is not a working day" (ss 322A(4), 327(3), 330(7), 360B(3)): encoded as the hours of the period less the hours that fall on a day that is not a working day, a figure the person states.
11. ss 316 (a statement for a general meeting; its first limb only for the annual general meeting of a public company), 340 (a public company's resolution for its annual general meeting) and 340B (a traded company's matters for its annual general meeting) are the same rule three times: expenses need not be paid if sufficient requests arrive before the end of the previous financial year; otherwise the members pay unless the company resolves otherwise, and the company is not bound to circulate unless a sum is deposited not later than a time before the meeting (one week in s 316(2)(b); six weeks or, if later, the time notice is given, in ss 340(2)(b) and 340B(2)(b)). The clear-day rule applies to the week (s 316(2)(b) is listed in s 360(1)) and to the six weeks (ss 340(2)(b)(i) and 340B(2)(b) are listed).
12. s 307(1) and (2) each exclude "an adjourned meeting"; the adjourned-meeting rule for a traded company is s 307A(7), and for a public or private company the Act in this range says nothing about the notice for an adjourned meeting (`NOTHING`, fork F19).
13. s 318(2) is the only subsection whose rule needs a count over persons rather than a fact about one person: two qualifying persons are a quorum unless each is only the representative of the same corporation or only the proxy of the same member. Tested in `coa7-tests-ch3c.l4`: two representatives of the same corporation are not a quorum, but are with one member present; two proxies of one member with a proxy of another member are a quorum.
14. s 288(1), s 282(1) and s 283(1) are definitions with no input the Act names (a vote count); the layer-1 `s 282 vote count` record carries the three figures the three ways of voting need. For a written resolution the denominator is the total voting rights of all eligible members, not the votes cast (s 282(2), s 283(2)); for a show of hands and a poll it is the votes cast (ss 282(3), (4), 283(4), (5)): a 40-for, 10-against, 100-eligible result passes an ordinary resolution on a show of hands and does not pass it as a written resolution (tested).
15. s 285A voids the poll-vote provision only for a resolution "required or authorised by an enactment" in a private company's articles; the rest of a private company's articles are untouched.
16. s 305(1) lets "the members who requested the meeting, or any of them representing more than one half of the total voting rights of all of them" call it: a minority subset answers `NOTHING` (limited by its own wording), not `JUST FALSE`.
17. The Act's own index (Schedule 8, lines 19644 and 19942 of the text) lists "annual general meeting | section 336" and "special notice, in relation to a resolution | section 312", but neither section defines the term: s 336 requires an annual general meeting and s 312 is headed "Resolution requiring special notice". Both have a defined-term row that points at the function that answers the nearest question.
18. **A silent trap in the language hit this encoding.** `IF NOT a AND b AND c` reads as `NOT (a AND b AND c)` (the brief warns of it). It hit `s 288(1)`: its tests failed on the first run, and every `NOT` in every module is now bracketed, `(NOT x)`. The check made for this part is a script that strips comments, quoted strings and backtick names from each code line and requires an opening bracket directly before every `NOT`: it reports 0 hits over every `coa7-*.l4` file. (A plain `grep -E` for this is unreliable with the macOS `grep`.)

## What the stable binary does not accept, and what it does

Measured on 2026-11-12 with `L4_BIN` (1.6.110, main, e51181d) while writing this part; the same files run identically on `L4_MAIN` (1.6.109): `check.sh` compares the counts of every module and goes `same`, and the regulative traces below give the same outcomes on both. None of the rejected spellings below appears in any module.

- **Regulative constructs cannot be asserted.** A `#TRACE` prints `FULFILLED`, a breach or a residual obligation and no assertion line, so `check.sh` cannot count it. Each of the five duties in `coa7-regulative.l4` therefore has its decision-function twin asserted beside it, and the traces are recorded below (identical on both builds).
- **Rejected or wrong, found by running (diagnostic codes read on 1.6.110, 2026-11-12):** `JUST Date 10 3 2026` without brackets is `check/wrong-argument-count` (`JUST` is applied to the four arguments; `JUST (Date 10 3 2026)` is required); `LIST "a" "b"` without a comma is `parse/unexpected-string` (`LIST "a", "b"`); a `MAYBE BOOLEAN` where a `BOOLEAN` is wanted, for example `(JUST TRUE) AND TRUE`, is `check/type-mismatch` (the spelling is `... EQUALS JUST TRUE`); a list of records written with `OF` needs each record in brackets (`LIST (`s 360C class of shares` OF TRUE, TRUE)`); and the silent one, `NOT a AND b` reading as `NOT (a AND b)` (finding 18).
- **Accepted and relied on (each is used in a module that compiles with no diagnostic on both builds):** `IMPORT` of a sibling module by file name in backticks, including a diamond (the seat imports `coa7-ch1` directly and through `coa7-ch2`, and `coa7-ch7` directly and through `coa7-ch5`); `add months` and `add years` on a `Date`; `date PLUS days` and `date MINUS date` (giving days); `isJust`, `elem`, `any`, `filter`, `map` with `GIVEN x YIELD ...`, `sort`, `take`, `append`; record equality with `MAYBE` fields (the core's `Penalty`) in `#ASSERT`; enumerations of this part and the core's `Mode of conviction` as `@export` inputs in the seat layer (`l4 check` accepts them).
- **Not measured:** the deployed behaviour of an `@export` function whose input is an enumeration or a list on Legalese Cloud (the seat layer checks and runs on both binaries; deployment is a person's step).
- No `REFUSE`, `#ASSERT REFUSED`, `BECAUSE` (except in `LEST BREACH BY ... BECAUSE`), sets, `TYPICALLY`, opaque `DECLARE`, section `GIVEN` or `YMD` is used anywhere; dates are `Date day month year`.

## The regulative rules and their traces

Five duties with a time limit in days, written `PARTY ... MUST ... WITHIN ... HENCE FULFILLED LEST BREACH BY ... BECAUSE "..."` over the core's `Action of a party`. `WITHIN n` counts from the moment the rule is entered, which is how each section counts for the duty it creates; a duty whose time limit counts from a date the person states is the decision-function twin's job. The outcomes below are the first line of the result printed by `l4 run` on each build (11 traces); the twin is asserted in the same file. The third trace of `s 304(1)(a)` has a different party (the company) performing the action: the obligation is left standing, shown as the residual `PARTY Director MUST ...`, and no breach is recorded; that is the only trace of the eleven that neither fulfils nor breaches.

| rule | event | on 1.6.110 | on 1.6.109 | decision-function twin |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `s 304(1)(a) the directors must call the meeting within 21 days` | PARTY Director DOES `Action of a party` OF Director, Member, "call the general meeting" AT 21 | fulfilled | fulfilled | `s 304(1)(a) the directors called the meeting in time` |
| `s 304(1)(a) the directors must call the meeting within 21 days` | PARTY Director DOES `Action of a party` OF Director, Member, "call the general meeting" AT 22 | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | `s 304(1)(a) the directors called the meeting in time` |
| `s 304(1)(a) the directors must call the meeting within 21 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "call the general meeting" AT 10 | residual obligation: PARTY Director MUST ... | residual obligation: PARTY Director MUST ... | `s 304(1)(a) the directors called the meeting in time` |
| `s 293(3) the company must send or submit the copies within 21 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "send or submit the copies of the resolution to every eligible member" AT 21 | fulfilled | fulfilled | `s 293(3) the copies were sent or submitted in time` |
| `s 293(3) the company must send or submit the copies within 21 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "send or submit the copies of the resolution to every eligible member" AT 22 | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | `s 293(3) the copies were sent or submitted in time` |
| `s 343(2) the directors must appoint the independent assessor within one week` | PARTY Director DOES `Action of a party` OF Director, Company, "appoint an independent assessor to prepare a report" AT 7 | fulfilled | fulfilled | `s 343(2) the appointment was made in time` |
| `s 343(2) the directors must appoint the independent assessor within one week` | PARTY Director DOES `Action of a party` OF Director, Company, "appoint an independent assessor to prepare a report" AT 8 | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | `s 343(2) the appointment was made in time` |
| `s 341(1B) the traded company must make the poll results available within 16 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "make the poll results available on a website" AT 15 | fulfilled | fulfilled | `s 341(1B) the traded company has complied with subsection (1A) in time` |
| `s 341(1B) the traded company must make the poll results available within 16 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "make the poll results available on a website" AT 16 | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | `s 341(1B) the traded company has complied with subsection (1A) in time` |
| `s 360BA(3) the traded company must provide the information within 15 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "provide information enabling the member to confirm that the vote was validly recorded and counted" AT 14 | fulfilled | fulfilled | `s 360BA(3) the information was provided in time` |
| `s 360BA(3) the traded company must provide the information within 15 days` | PARTY Company DOES `Action of a party` OF Company, Member, "provide information enabling the member to confirm that the vote was validly recorded and counted" AT 15 | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | breach (DEONTIC BREACHED) | `s 360BA(3) the information was provided in time` |

## The seat layer

170 questions in `coa7-seat.l4`, grouped by chapter under `§` headings in the Act's order, each with `@export` and a one-sentence question, and an `@desc` on every input worded as the person would be asked. Plain inputs only: yes/no, numbers, dates, hours, a choice from a list or a core type, and for `s 286` a list of names. The layer-1 records (`s 282 vote count`, `s 318 person present`, `s 360C class of shares`) are built inside the question from those inputs. The person never states a seam answer as a `MAYBE`: a fact another part decides (whether an officer is "in default", whether a company is "quoted") is asked as the yes/no the person can give. Officials' powers appear only as what they mean to the person ("may the court order a meeting on my application", "what penalty could I face"). `coa7-cases-seat.l4` has 462 assertions: at least one case for every question, in the facts a director, secretary or member would state, with the `NOTHING` cases ("the Act does not decide") asserted. A penalty question answers the core's `Penalty` (level and fraction of a level, never pounds). Every section of the range has at least one question except s 354, which addresses only the Secretary of State's regulation-making power (inert, reason 2). On this machine `l4 check` of the seat layer took 1.5 s on 1.6.110 and 1.4 s on 1.6.109.

### Seat audit of 2026-11-12

The lead asked for every seat question's name and `@export` text to be read against the layer-1 function its body calls. All 170 were read. 17 named something other than the function answers: 7 by direction (the name asked a permission, or "need I", where the function answers the bar or the reverse, so a reader would take `JUST TRUE` the wrong way) and 10 by subject (the name asked a different thing from the function: "without a deposit" for "given any deposit or resolution", "in time" for "meets the requirements", "must keep" for "has kept"). Each is retitled to ask what the function answers; no answer was negated, and no expected value changed. The 53 assertions that mention a retitled question are restated in place in `coa7-cases-seat.l4`, each with a `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was ...` line above it, and each retitled question carries a `-- RECONCILED` line in `coa7-seat.l4`. The other 153 questions were read and match their functions.

| kind | the name before | the name now | why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| direction | `Under section 285, do my proxies together have more voting rights than I would in person` | `Under section 285, does section 285(3) authorise my proxies together to exercise more voting rights than I could in person` | the old name asked a comparison of fact (TRUE for more) but s 285(4) answers whether subsection (3) authorises it (JUST FALSE for more) |
| subject | `Under section 294, is the company bound to circulate the resolution without a deposit` | `Under section 294, is the company bound to circulate the resolution, given any deposit or its own resolution to meet the expenses` | the old name said "without a deposit", but the function answers whether the company is bound given what it resolved or what was deposited (JUST FALSE when neither) |
| direction | `Under section 316, need the members who requested the circulation pay the company's expenses` | `Under section 316, are the members who requested the circulation excused from paying the company's expenses` | the old name asked whether they need pay, but s 316(1) answers that they need NOT pay (JUST TRUE means excused) |
| subject | `Under section 316, is the company bound to circulate the statement without a deposit` | `Under section 316, is the company bound to circulate the statement, given any deposit or its own resolution to meet the expenses` | the old name said "without a deposit", but the function answers whether the company is bound given what it resolved or what was deposited (JUST FALSE when neither) |
| direction | `Under section 321, can the articles make my demand for a poll ineffective` | `Under section 321, is a provision of the articles void in so far as it would make my demand for a poll ineffective` | the old name asked a permission (can the articles) but s 321(2) answers that the provision is void, a bar |
| direction | `Under section 322, need I use all my votes on a poll in the same way` | `Under section 322, may I use only some of my votes on a poll, or cast them in different ways` | the old name asked whether I need use all my votes, but s 322 answers that the member need NOT |
| subject | `Under section 326, were the proxy invitations issued in breach of the Act` | `Under section 326, do the company's proxy invitations contravene subsection (1)` | the old name said breach of the Act, but s 326(1) answers whether the invitations contravene that subsection only |
| direction | `Under section 330, does the termination of my proxy's authority affect his vote` | `Under section 330, does the termination of my proxy's authority leave the vote my proxy gave valid` | the old name asked whether the termination affects the vote, but s 330(3) answers that it does NOT affect its validity (JUST TRUE means valid) |
| direction | `Under section 331, may the articles give members or proxies more rights than sections 324 to 330` | `Under section 331, do sections 324 to 330 prevent my company's articles from giving members or proxies more extensive rights` | the old name asked a permission (may the articles) but s 331 answers whether sections 324 to 330 prevent it, a bar (JUST FALSE means they do not) |
| subject | `Under section 338, did we make our request for the annual general meeting resolution in time` | `Under section 338, does our request for the annual general meeting resolution meet the requirements, including the time` | the old name asked only about time, but s 338(4) answers whether the request meets all the requirements of the subsection |
| subject | `Under section 340, is the public company bound to circulate our resolution without a deposit` | `Under section 340, is the public company bound to circulate our resolution, given any deposit or its own resolution to meet the expenses` | the old name said "without a deposit", but the function answers whether the company is bound given what it resolved or what was deposited (JUST FALSE when neither) |
| subject | `Under section 340A, has the traded company given notice of our matter and published it` | `Under section 340A, has the traded company given notice of our matter in the way the Act requires` | the old name asked about notice and publication, but s 340A(1)(a) answers notice only (publication is s 340A(1)(b), its own question) |
| subject | `Under section 340B, is the traded company bound to circulate our matter without a deposit` | `Under section 340B, is the traded company bound to circulate our matter, given any deposit or its own resolution to meet the expenses` | the old name said "without a deposit", but the function answers whether the company is bound given what it resolved or what was deposited (JUST FALSE when neither) |
| subject | `Under section 342, may the members of a quoted company require an independent report on a poll` | `Under section 342, are the directors required to obtain an independent report on a poll because enough members asked` | the old name asked about the members' power (s 342(1)), but the function answers s 342(2), whether the directors are required |
| subject | `Under section 355, must my company keep this record` | `Under section 355, has my company kept this record` | the old name asked about the duty, but s 355(1) as encoded answers whether the company has kept the record |
| direction | `Under section 360A, may a meeting be held so that members attend electronically` | `Under section 360A, does Part 13 stop a meeting being held so that members attend electronically` | the old name asked a permission (may a meeting be held) but s 360A(1) answers whether this Part precludes it, a bar (JUST FALSE means it does not) |
| subject | `Under section 360B, may the traded company fix the time for who may vote` | `Under section 360B, is the time the traded company fixed for who may vote one the Act allows` | the old name asked a general permission, but s 360B(2) answers whether the time actually fixed is one the subsection allows |

## What `check.sh` printed

Run as `L4=<L4_BIN> L4_MAIN=<L4_MAIN> ./check.sh` from this folder (target `main`: every module is also run with the second build and the counts compared). The baseline it replaced is the keel's: 3 modules, 346 assertions (`coa-core.l4`, `coa-definitions.l4`, `coa-definitions-tests.l4`); this folder carries the keel's two modules `coa-core.l4` and `coa-definitions.l4` and not its test file, so those two print zeros here and the keel's 346 are not in this run. `expected_failed` is empty: no assertion is meant to fail.

Beyond it: `python3 -I srcrefs.py check --strict ukpga-2006-46.txt coa7-*.l4` printed `srcrefs check: 809 src: lines, 378 runs, 390 locators, 0 problems` over every module of this part, and `--refs` printed the same line; `l4 check` of each module prints no `@ref ... follows code on its line` and no `@ref ... is at the end of the file` line.

## Open questions for a domain expert

1. F5 (a bare "must" not done answers JUST FALSE) is used at 54 sites: is a requirement stated with "must" and no stated consequence a "no" to everything else?
2. F2: is "simple majority" more than one half, and is a tie a defeat?
3. F7: does the first day count in "within N days from D" and in "the period of N days beginning with D"? The Act has no counting rule outside s 360.
4. F3 and F8: should an "unless" bar also be answered positively (the resolution IS special once stated; the resolution IS validly sent once available)? The encoding answers the bar only.
5. s 281(1), (2) and s 281(4): is a resolution passed in none of the ways s 281 allows void, or only not "passed under this Part" (fork F1)?
6. s 318(2): is one qualifying person at a meeting of a company that has more than one member a quorum under the articles only?
7. s 343(3): the function answers `NOTHING` for a person who meets the independence requirement and has no other role (the subsection says only whom the directors must not appoint). Is that the intended reading?
8. s 346(2): when a contrary intention appears, is the appointment of the partners (not the firm)?
9. s 344(2): a person who is neither an auditor nor otherwise an officer or employee answers `JUST FALSE` (the person is simply not one); is `NOTHING` (the text speaks only of auditors) the better answer? It decides what `Under section 344, may this person not be appointed ...` says for a stranger with no other connection.

## NEEDS-CORE, copied modules, peeked

No `NEEDS-CORE.md`: every type and constructor this part needed is declared here under the `s N` prefix (`s 282 vote count`, `s 318 person present`, `s 360C class of shares`, the enumerations for the choices of each section), and the core's `Kind of resolution`, `Way a resolution is passed`, `Mode of conviction`, `Penalty`, `Fine limit`, `Period` and `Unit of time` were enough. No module was copied from another encoding. Peeked: nothing (no existing encoding, no runs directory but `dst-coa7`, nothing under `<local tool>` was opened by this part).
